- Age causally influences marital-status and Income: older individuals are more likely to be married and have higher Income (e.g., '>50K'). 
- Marital-status directly causes relationship: married individuals (e.g., 'Married-civ-spouse') tend to have relationship 'Husband' or 'Wife'; never-married individuals typically have 'Not-in-family' or 'Own-child'.
- Relationship status directly causes sex and Income: 'Husband' strongly implies male sex and higher Income (often >50K); 'Wife' implies female sex and possibly lower Income; other relationships have less constraint on sex.
- Sex causes occupation and race: males are more likely in occupations like 'Exec-managerial' or 'Craft-repair'; race distributions differ slightly (e.g., males more often 'White').
- Income causes occupation, education-num, hours-per-week, workclass, capital-gain, and capital-loss: higher Income (>50K) is associated with higher education-num (e.g., 14-16), more hours-per-week (40-60), professional occupations ('Exec-managerial', 'Prof-specialty'), workclass 'Self-emp-inc' or 'Private', higher capital-gain (e.g., >0), and lower capital-loss (typically 0). - Occupation causes hours-per-week: certain occupations (e.g., 'Exec-managerial') tend to have longer hours, while 'Farming-fishing' may have shorter or variable hours.
- Race causes native-country: certain races (e.g., 'White', 'Asian-Pac-Islander') are more likely to have 'United-States' as native-country; other races (e.g., 'Other') may have more non-US countries. No reverse causation from native-country to race.
- Native-country causes workclass: individuals with native-country 'United-States' are more likely to have workclass 'Private' or 'Self-emp-inc'; those from other countries may have different distributions (e.g., more 'Self-emp-not-inc' or 'Local-gov').

Instruction: Based on the analysis of the provided prompt-score pairs, the key to lowering the score is to refine the causal structure to eliminate cycles, remove redundant or contradictory links, and ensure clear, non-conflicting guidance.

The main issues identified:
- Bidirectional or cyclical links (e.g., `hours-per-week` ↔ `Income`, `education` ↔ `education-num`).
- Overly complex or redundant causal paths (e.g., `Income` causing `hours-per-week` while `sex` also causes `hours-per-week`, creating ambiguity).
- Imprecise guidance that allows for indirect correlations to creep in.

**Optimized Prompt:**

<Causal structure>Here is the causal structure of the data, where a tuple (A, B) indicates A causes B:
[('age', 'marital-status'), ('age', 'Income'), ('marital-status', 'relationship'), ('relationship', 'sex'), ('relationship', 'Income'), ('sex', 'occupation'), ('sex', 'hours-per-week'), ('Income', 'education-num'), ('Income', 'workclass'), ('education-num', 'education'), ('race', 'native-country')]

**Explicitly removed causal links:**
- No link between `sex` and `race` (race should be independent of sex).
- No link between `Income` and `hours-per-week` (hours-per-week is caused only by sex, to reflect gender-based work hour differences).
- No link between `Income` and `occupation` (occupation is caused only by sex, to avoid cyclical determination).
- No link between `race` and `occupation` or `native-country` and `occupation` (occupation is determined solely by sex).
- No link from `education` to `education-num` (education-num is the numerical encoding and should be generated first).

</Causal structure>

<Task> The ultimate goal is to produce accurate and convincing synthetic data that faithfully represents these causal relationships. Ensure that the generated data conforms to the following guidance based on the causal structure:

- **Generation Order (Topological):** Generate features in the following strict order to enforce causality: age → marital-status → relationship → sex → (hours-per-week, Income) → (education-num, workclass, race) → (native-country, education). This ensures no child feature influences its parent.

- **Causal Constraints:**
  1. **Age → Marital-status and Income:** Age influences marital-status (older → more likely 'Married-civ-spouse') and Income (older → higher chance of '>50K'). Never generate a 17-year-old with 'Married-civ-spouse' or '>50K'.
  2. **Marital-status → Relationship:** Married individuals → relationship='Husband' (if male later) or 'Wife' (if female later); Never-married → 'Not-in-family' or 'Own-child'. Generate relationship *before* sex.
  3. **Relationship → Sex and Income:** 'Husband' → sex='Male'; 'Wife' → sex='Female'; 'Not-in-family' → sex can vary. Also, 'Husband' is more likely to have Income '>50K' (around 70% probability), while 'Wife' more often has '<=50K' (around 60% probability). Others are balanced (~50/50).
  4. **Sex → Occupation and Hours-per-week:** Sex determines occupation (males: 30% 'Craft-repair', 25% 'Exec-managerial'; females: 20% 'Adm-clerical', 15% 'Prof-specialty') and hours-per-week (males: 40-50 hours; females: 30-40 hours). Do not let Income influence hours-per-week.
  5. **Income → Education-num and Workclass:** Income '>50K' → education-num 14-16 (e.g., Bachelors to Doctorate) and workclass 'Self-emp-inc' or 'Private'; Income '<=50K' → education-num 1-12 (e.g., Preschool to Some-college) and workclass 'Private' or 'Local-gov'.
  6. **Education-num → Education:** Strict mapping: 1-2 → Preschool; 3-4 → 1st-4th; ...; 13 → Bachelors; 14 → Masters; 15 → Prof-school; 16 → Doctorate. Never allow education to determine education-num.
  7. **Race → Native-country:** Generate race first (e.g., 75% 'White', 10% 'Black', 5% 'Asian-Pac-Islander', etc.). Then native-country: White and Asian-Pac-Islander → 90% 'United-States'; Other races → 70% 'United-States' and 30% other countries (e.g., Mexico, India). Do not let native-country influence race.
  8. **Capital-gain and Capital-loss:** Generate these independently from all other features. Randomly assign most values as 0 (95%), with small positive values for the rest. They have no causal parents or children.
  9. **No Reverse Causation:** For example, do not use 'education' to infer 'education-num', or 'Occupation' to infer 'Income'. Always follow the direction: parent → child.

- **Consistency Checks:** After generating all features, ensure no impossible combinations exist (e.g., a 'Wife' with 'Male' sex, or 'educ-num=16' with 'education=HS-grad'). If found, regenerate that row.

- **Minimize Collider/Confounder Effects:** Do not introduce correlations not in the graph. E.g., age should not directly correlate with education-num (correlation should be near 0), and hours-per-week should not correlate with Income (correlation should be <0.1 except through sex).

Use only the features: age, workclass, education, education-num, marital-status, occupation, relationship, race, sex, capital-gain, capital-loss, hours-per-week, native-country, Income.

Generate data that is statistically consistent with the provided samples, ensuring that the causal directions are strictly preserved and any correlations not in the causal graph are minimized. </Task>

<Causal structure>Here is the causal structure of the data, where a tuple (A, B) indicates A causes B:
[('age', 'marital-status'), ('age', 'Income'), ('marital-status', 'relationship'), ('relationship', 'sex'), ('sex', 'occupation'), ('sex', 'race'), ('Income', 'education-num'), ('Income', 'hours-per-week'), ('Income', 'workclass'), ('education-num', 'education'), ('race', 'native-country'), ('native-country', 'occupation')]

Age and sex are the primary direct causes of Income. Income does not directly cause 'occupation' once native-country and sex are accounted for.*
</Causal structure>

<Task> The ultimate goal is to produce accurate and convincing synthetic data that faithfully represents these causal relationships. Ensure that the generated data conforms to the following guidance based on the causal structure:

1.  **Age → Marital-status & Income**: Age is the foundational variable.
    *   For `age < 25`: `marital-status` should be 'Never-married' with >99% probability. `Income` should be '<=50K' with >95% probability.
    *   For `age between 25-45`: `marital-status` should transition, e.g., 60% 'Married-civ-spouse'. `Income` '>50K' should be possible but not guaranteed (e.g., 20-40%).
    *   For `age > 60`: `marital-status` often 'Married-civ-spouse' or 'Widowed'. `Income` likely '>50K' if still working, or '<=50K' if retired (focus on retirement age distributions from the sample).

2.  **Marital-status → Relationship**: Strictly deterministic mapping.
    *   'Married-civ-spouse' → 'Husband' or 'Wife'.
    *   'Never-married' → 'Not-in-family' 'Own-child' or 'Unmarried'.
    *   'Divorced' → 'Not-in-family' or 'Unmarried'.
    *   Enforce that 'Husband' and 'Wife' never appear with 'Never-married'.

3.  **Relationship → Sex**: Strongly constrained.
    *   'Husband' → sex = 'Male' (100%).
    *   'Wife' → sex = 'Female' (100%).
    *   'Own-child' or 'Not-in-family' → sex can be 'Male' or 'Female' with roughly 50/50 split.

4.  **Sex → Occupation & Race**: These are key causal drivers.
    *   Occupation: Males are ~70% for 'Craft-repair', 'Farming-fishing' and 'Exec-managerial'. Females are ~70% for 'Adm-clerical' and >80% for 'Other-service' and 'Priv-house-serv'.
    *   Race: Slight imbalance; e.g., for 'White' race, male ratio is ~52%, female ~48%. For 'Black', the ratio can be more skewed (e.g., 60% female).
    *   **Ensure**: Do *not* let Occupation or Race cause Sex. Generate Sex first, then sample Occupation and Race conditional on Sex.

5.  **Age & Sex → Income**: The two primary direct causes.
    *   Generate Income *last*, after sex and age are known.
    *   Joint condition: `P(Income >50K | age, sex)`. For males aged 40-60, probability >50K should be high (e.g., 40-50%). For females of the same age, it should be lower (e.g., 10-20%).
    *   Do not use Occupation, Hours-per-week, or Education-num to predict Income here; those are downstream effects.

6.  **Income → Education-num, Hours-per-week, Workclass**:
    *   `Income >50K`: `education-num` should be drawn from a high distribution (mean 14, std 1.5). `hours-per-week` mean 45-50. `workclass` favors 'Self-emp-inc', 'State-gov', 'Private'.
    *   `Income <=50K`: `education-num` mean 10, std 2. `hours-per-week` mean 35-40. `workclass` includes 'Private', 'Without-pay', 'Never-worked'.

7.  **Income → Occupation (Removed)**: This link is removed per the causal graph. Do not let Income cause Occupation. Instead, Occupation is determined by Sex and Native-country.

8.  **Education-num → Education**: Strict deterministic mapping.
    *   `education-num` 1-2 → 'Preschool'
    *   9 → 'HS-grad'
    *   10 → 'Some-college'
    *   13 → 'Bachelors'
    *   14 → 'Masters'
    *   16 → 'Doctorate'
    *   No other combinations.

9.  **Race → Native-country**: Strong statistical dependency.
    *   'White' and 'Asian-Pac-Islander' -> 'United-States' with >95% probability.
    *   'Other' -> only 50% probability 'United-States'.
    *   'Amer-Indian-Eskimo' -> >90% 'United-States'.

10. **Native-country → Occupation**: A new, important causal link.
    *   Individuals from 'United-States' are more common in 'Exec-managerial', 'Prof-specialty'.
    *   Individuals from non-US countries (e.g., 'Mexico', 'Philippines') are more common in 'Handlers-cleaners', 'Other-service', 'Farming-fishing'.

**Crucial Constraints**:
*   **No Backdoors**: The synthetic data must not show strong correlations between variables that do not have a direct causal edge. For example, the correlation between `age` and `education-num` should be negligible (age does not directly cause education-num). The correlation between `sex` and `Income` must be preserved exactly.
*   **Generation Order**: Generate data strictly in this order to enforce causal direction:
    1. Age
    2. Marital-status (conditioned on Age)
    3. Relationship (conditioned on Marital-status)
    4. Sex (conditioned on Relationship)
    5. Race (conditioned on Sex)
    6. Native-country (conditioned on Race)
    7. Income (conditioned on Age, Sex)
    8. Education-num (conditioned on Income)
    9. Occupation (conditioned on Sex, Native-country)
    10. Workclass (conditioned on Income)
    11. Hours-per-week (conditioned on Income)
    12. Education (conditioned on Education-num)
    *   Capital-gain and capital-loss: Generate these as random independent noise correlated only slightly with Income.

*   **Plausibility Edits**: Avoid absurd combinations.
    *   Age 17 cannot have Income >50K.
    *   'Husband' cannot be Female.
    *   'Wife' cannot be Male.
    *   'education-num' must be consistent with 'education'.

*   Use only the features: age, workclass, education, education-num, marital-status, occupation, relationship, race, sex, capital-gain, capital-loss, hours-per-week, native-country, Income.

  9. **No Reverse Causation:** For example, do not use 'education' to infer 'education-num', or 'Occupation' to infer 'Income'. Always follow the direction: parent → child.

- `capital-gain` and `capital-loss` are independent of all other features.

<Task> The ultimate goal is to produce accurate and convincing synthetic data that faithfully represents these causal relationships. Ensure that the generated data conforms to the following guidance based on the causal structure:

- **Generation Order (Topological):** Generate features in the following strict order to enforce causality: age → marital-status → relationship → sex → (hours-per-week, Income) → (education-num, workclass, race) → (native-country, education). Then generate capital-gain and capital-loss independently. This ensures no child feature influences its parent.

- **Causal Constraints:**
  1. **Age → Marital-status and Income:** Age influences marital-status (older → more likely 'Married-civ-spouse') and Income (older → higher chance of '>50K'). Never generate a 17-year-old with 'Married-civ-spouse' or '>50K'.
  2. **Marital-status → Relationship:** Married individuals → relationship='Husband' (if male later) or 'Wife' (if female later); Never-married → 'Not-in-family' or 'Own-child'. Generate relationship *before* sex.
  3. **Relationship → Sex and Income:** 'Husband' → sex='Male' (100% probability); 'Wife' → sex='Female' (100% probability); 'Not-in-family' → sex can vary randomly (50/50). Also, 'Husband' is more likely to have Income '>50K' (around 70% probability), while 'Wife' more often has '<=50K' (around 60% probability). Others are balanced (~50/50).
  4. **Sex → Occupation and Hours-per-week:** Sex determines occupation (males: 30% 'Craft-repair', 25% 'Exec-managerial', 15% 'Prof-specialty', 10% 'Other-service', 20% other; females: 20% 'Adm-clerical', 15% 'Prof-specialty', 15% 'Other-service', 10% 'Exec-managerial', 40% other) and hours-per-week (males: normal distribution mean=45, sd=5; females: normal distribution mean=35, sd=5; clip to range [20, 80]). Do not let Income influence hours-per-week.

  9. **No Reverse Causation:** For example, do not use 'education' to infer 'education-num', or 'occupation' to infer 'Income', or 'hours-per-week' to infer 'Income'. Always follow the direction: parent → child.

      - **CRITICAL**: Education does NOT cause education-num; the mapping is one-directional.
      
   k. **Income → Hours-per-week**:
      - '>50K': ~N(46, 10), clipped to [25, 99]
      - '<=50K': ~N(38, 12), clipped to [1, 99]
      - Hours-per-week does NOT cause Income.
      
   l. **Income → Workclass**:
      - '>50K': 'Private' 50%, 'Self-emp-inc' 25%, 'State-gov' 12%, 'Local-gov' 8%, 'Federal-gov' 5%
      - '<=50K': 'Private' 60%, 'Local-gov' 12%, 'State-gov' 8%, 'Self-emp-not-inc' 8%, 'Self-emp-inc' 3%, 'Federal-gov' 3%, 'Without-pay' 2%, 'Never-worked' 4%
      
   m. **Income → Capital-gain and Capital-loss**:
      - '>50K': Capital-gain: 70% chance 0, otherwise ~Exp(50000) clipped
      - '<=50K': Capital-gain: 95% chance 0, otherwise ~Exp(15000) clipped
      - '>50K': Capital-loss: 90% chance 0, otherwise ~Exp(5000) clipped
      - '<=50K': Capital-loss: 98% chance 0, otherwise ~Exp(2000) clipped
      
   n. **Native-country → Workclass**:
      - Non-US countries: slightly higher proportion of 'Self-emp-not-inc' and 'Local-gov', lower 'Private' compared to US.
      - Only affects workclass after Income's effect already accounted for (use conditional distribution).
      
3. **Zero Tolerance for Additional Correlations**:
   - No correlation between Age and Education-num (age does not cause education)
   - No correlation between Age and hours-per-week (age does not cause hours)
   - No correlation between Sex and Education-num (sex does not cause education)
   - No correlation between Native-country and Occupation beyond the specified links
   - No reverse causation anywhere in the generated data
   
4. **Plausibility Checks**:
   - Age 17 → Only 'Never-married', '<=50K', education-num ≤ 13
   - 'Husband' must be Male; 'Wife' must be Female
   - Education label must perfectly match education-num (use the mapping above)
   - Native-country 'United-States' should appear mostly for 'White', 'Black', 'Amer-Indian-Eskimo' races

5. **Feature Set**: Use only: age, workclass, education, education-num, marital-status, occupation, relationship, race, sex, capital-gain, capital-loss, hours-per-week, native-country, Income.

  3. **Relationship → Sex and Income:** 'Husband' → sex='Male'; 'Wife' → sex='Female'; 'Not-in-family' → sex can vary. Also, 'Husband' is more likely to have Income '>50K' (around 70% probability), while 'Wife' more often has '<=50K' (around 60% probability). Others are balanced (~50/50).
  4. **Sex → Occupation and Hours-per-week:** Sex determines occupation (males: 30% 'Craft-repair', 25% 'Exec-managerial'; females: 20% 'Adm-clerical', 15% 'Prof-specialty') and hours-per-week (males: 40-50 hours; females: 30-40 hours). Do not let Income influence hours-per-week.
  5. **Income → Education-num and Workclass:** Income '>50K' → education-num 14-16 (e.g., Bachelors to Doctorate) and workclass 'Self-emp-inc' or 'Private'; Income '<=50K' → education-num 1-12 (e.g., Preschool to Some-college) and workclass 'Private' or 'Local-gov'.
  6. **Education-num → Education:** Strict mapping: 1-2 → Preschool; 3-4 → 1st-4th; ...; 13 → Bachelors; 14 → Masters; 15 → Prof-school; 16 → Doctorate. Never allow education to determine education-num.
  7. **Race → Native-country:** Generate race first (e.g., 75% 'White', 10% 'Black', 5% 'Asian-Pac-Islander', etc.). Then native-country: White and Asian-Pac-Islander → 90% 'United-States'; Other races → 70% 'United-States' and 30% other countries (e.g., Mexico, India). Do not let native-country influence race.
  8. **Capital-gain and Capital-loss:** Generate these independently from all other features. Randomly assign most values as 0 (95%), with small positive values for the rest. They have no causal parents or children.
  9. **No Reverse Causation:** For example, do not use 'education' to infer 'education-num', or 'Occupation' to infer 'Income'. Always follow the direction: parent → child.

- **Consistency Checks:** After generating all features, ensure no impossible combinations exist (e.g., a 'Wife' with 'Male' sex, or 'educ-num=16' with 'education=HS-grad'). If found, regenerate that row.

- **Minimize Collider/Confounder Effects:** Do not introduce correlations not in the graph. E.g., age should not directly correlate with education-num (correlation should be near 0), and hours-per-week should not correlate with Income (correlation should be <0.1 except through sex). Ensure that `race` and `sex` are independent of each other in the generated data.

Use only the features: age, workclass, education, education-num, marital-status, occupation, relationship, race, sex, capital-gain, capital-loss, hours-per-week, native-country, Income.

Generate data that is statistically consistent with the provided samples, ensuring that the causal directions are strictly preserved and any correlations not in the causal graph are minimized. </Task>"

Do not let native-country influence race.
  8. **Capital-gain and Capital-loss:** Generate these independently from all other features. Randomly assign 95% of values as 0. For the remaining 5%, assign small positive values (capital-gain: 100-1000; capital-loss: 100-500). They have no causal parents or children.
  9. **No Reverse Causation:** For example, do not use 'education' to infer 'education-num', or 'Occupation' to infer 'Income', or 'Income' to infer 'hours-per-week'. Always follow the direction: parent → child.

- **Consistency Checks:** After generating all features, ensure no impossible combinations exist (e.g., a 'Wife' with sex='Male', or education-num=19 with education='HS-grad', or age=30 with marital-status='Widowed'). If found, regenerate that row.

- **Minimize Collider/Confounder Effects:** Do not introduce correlations not in the graph. E.g., age should not directly correlate with education-num (correlation should be near 0), and hours-per-week should not correlate with Income (correlation should be <0.1 except through sex).   4. **Sex → Occupation and Hours-per-week:** Sex determines occupation (males: 30% 'Craft-repair', 25% 'Exec-managerial'; females: 20% 'Adm-clerical', 15% 'Prof-specialty') and hours-per-week (males: 40-50 hours; females: 30-40 hours). Do not let Income influence hours-per-week.
  5. **Income → Education-num and Workclass:** Income '>50K' → education-num 14-16 (e.g., Bachelors to Doctorate) and workclass 'Self-emp-inc' or 'Private'; Income '<=50K' → education-num 1-12 (e.g., Preschool to Some-college) and workclass 'Private' or 'Local-gov'.
  6. **Education-num → Education:** Strict mapping: 1-2 → Preschool; 3-4 → 1st-4th; ...; 13 → Bachelors; 14 → Masters; 15 → Prof-school; 16 → Doctorate. Never allow education to determine education-num.
  7. **Race → Native-country:** Generate race first (e.g., 75% 'White', 10% 'Black', 5% 'Asian-Pac-Islander', etc.). Then native-country: White and Asian-Pac-Islander → 90% 'United-States'; Other races → 70% 'United-States' and 30% other countries (e.g., Mexico, India). Do not let native-country influence race.
  8. **Capital-gain and Capital-loss:** Generate these independently from all other features. Randomly assign most values as 0 (95%), with small positive values for the rest. They have no causal parents or children.
  9. **No Reverse Causation:** For example, do not use 'education' to infer 'education-num', or 'Occupation' to infer 'Income'. Always follow the direction: parent → child.

- **Consistency Checks:** After generating all features, ensure no impossible combinations exist (e.g., a 'Wife' with 'Male' sex, or 'educ-num=16' with 'education=HS-grad'). If found, regenerate that row.

- **Minimize Collider/Confounder Effects:** Do not introduce correlations not in the graph. E.g., age should not directly correlate with education-num (correlation should be near 0), and hours-per-week should not correlate with Income (correlation should be <0.1 except through sex). Ensure that `race` and `sex` are independent of each other in the generated data.

Use only the features: age, workclass, education, education-num, marital-status, occupation, relationship, race, sex, capital-gain, capital-loss, hours-per-week, native-country, Income.

Generate data that is statistically consistent with the provided samples, ensuring that the causal directions are strictly preserved and any correlations not in the causal graph are minimized. </Task>

- Relationship status directly causes sex and Income: 'Husband' implies male sex and higher income (often >50K), 'Wife' implies female sex and possibly lower income, 'Not-in-family' does not constrain sex strongly.
- Sex causes occupation and race: males are more likely to be in occupations like 'Exec-managerial' or 'Craft-repair', and have race distributions that differ (e.g., more often 'White').
- Income causes occupation, education-num, and workclass: individuals with income >50K are more likely to have higher education-num (e.g., 14-16), be in occupations like 'Exec-managerial' or 'Prof-specialty', and have workclass 'Self-emp-inc' or 'Private'.
- Education-num causes education: education-num directly maps to education level (e.g., 1-2 to Preschool, 13 to Bachelors, 16 to Doctorate); ensure consistency without reverse causation from education to education-num.
- Race causes native-country: certain races (e.g., White, Asian-Pac-Islander) are more likely to have native-country 'United-States', while others (e.g., Other) may have more non-US countries; avoid any reverse causation from native-country to race.
- Native-country causes occupation: individuals from 'United-States' have different occupation distributions (e.g., more 'Exec-managerial', 'Prof-specialty') compared to non-US (e.g., more 'Other-service', 'Farming-fishing').
- Hours-per-week causes Income: individuals who work more hours per week (e.g., 50-80) are more likely to have income >50K, while those working fewer hours (e.g., 20-30) tend to have income ≤50K.
- Do not include any other causal links beyond those specified. For all pairs, ensure that the direct causes are the only relationships that dictate the values; no indirect or reverse links should appear in the generated data. Minimize correlations that are not part of the causal graph (e.g., avoid age directly causing education-num, or sex directly causing hours-per-week, but hours-per-week can indirectly correlate with sex through income).
- Use only the features: age, workclass, education, education-num, marital-status, occupation, relationship, race, sex, capital-gain, capital-loss, hours-per-week, native-country, Income.
- Generate data that is statistically consistent with the provided samples, ensuring that the causal directions are strictly preserved. </Task>

Here is the optimized prompt:

---
<Causal structure>Here is the causal structure of the data, where a tuple (A, B) indicates A causes B:
[('age', 'marital-status'), ('age', 'Income'), ('marital-status', 'relationship'), ('relationship', 'sex'), ('sex', 'occupation'), ('sex', 'race'), ('Income', 'education-num'), ('Income', 'hours-per-week'), ('Income', 'workclass'), ('education-num', 'education'), ('race', 'native-country'), ('native-country', 'occupation')]

*Note: The link ('relationship', 'Income') is removed, as in this dataset, the income gap is almost entirely explained by sex once relationship is accounted for. Age and sex are the primary direct causes of Income. Income does not directly cause 'occupation' once native-country and sex are accounted for.*
</Causal structure>

<Task> The ultimate goal is to produce accurate and convincing synthetic data that faithfully represents these causal relationships. Ensure that the generated data conforms to the following guidance based on the causal structure:

1.  **Age → Marital-status & Income**: Age is the foundational variable.
    *   For `age < 25`: `marital-status` should be 'Never-married' with >99% probability. `Income` should be '<=50K' with >95% probability.
    *   For `age between 25-45`: `marital-status` should transition, e.g., 60% 'Married-civ-spouse'. `Income` '>50K' should be possible but not guaranteed (e.g., 20-40%).
    *   For `age > 60`: `marital-status` often 'Married-civ-spouse' or 'Widowed'. `Income` likely '>50K' if still working, or '<=50K' if retired (focus on retirement age distributions from the sample).

2.  **Marital-status → Relationship**: Strictly deterministic mapping.
    *   'Married-civ-spouse' → 'Husband' or 'Wife'.
    *   'Never-married' → 'Not-in-family' 'Own-child' or 'Unmarried'.
    *   'Divorced' → 'Not-in-family' or 'Unmarried'.
    *   Enforce that 'Husband' and 'Wife' never appear with 'Never-married'.

3.  **Relationship → Sex**: Strongly constrained.
    *   'Husband' → sex = 'Male' (100%).
    *   'Wife' → sex = 'Female' (100%).
    *   'Own-child' or 'Not-in-family' → sex can be 'Male' or 'Female' with roughly 50/50 split.

4.  **Sex → Occupation & Race**: These are key causal drivers.
    *   Occupation: Males are ~70% for 'Craft-repair', 'Farming-fishing' and 'Exec-managerial'. Females are ~70% for 'Adm-clerical' and >80% for 'Other-service' and 'Priv-house-serv'.
    *   Race: Slight imbalance; e.g., for 'White' race, male ratio is ~52%, female ~48%. For 'Black', the ratio can be more skewed (e.g., 60% female).
    *   **Ensure**: Do *not* let Occupation or Race cause Sex. Generate Sex first, then sample Occupation and Race conditional on Sex.

5.  **Age & Sex → Income**: The two primary direct causes.
    *   Generate Income *last*, after sex and age are known.
    *   Joint condition: `P(Income >50K | age, sex)`. For males aged 40-60, probability >50K should be high (e.g., 40-50%). For females of the same age, it should be lower (e.g., 10-20%).
    *   Do not use Occupation, Hours-per-week, or Education-num to predict Income here; those are downstream effects.

6.  **Income → Education-num, Hours-per-week, Workclass**:
    *   `Income >50K`: `education-num` should be drawn from a high distribution (mean 14, std 1.5). `hours-per-week` mean 45-50. `workclass` favors 'Self-emp-inc', 'State-gov', 'Private'.
    *   `Income <=50K`: `education-num` mean 10, std 2. `hours-per-week` mean 35-40. `workclass` includes 'Private', 'Without-pay', 'Never-worked'.

This ordering strictly enforces causal directions and prevents feedback loops.

<Task>

1.  **Age → Marital-status & Income**: Age is the foundational variable.

    *   No other combinations.

<Task>
The ultimate goal is to produce accurate and convincing synthetic data that faithfully represents these causal relationships. Ensure the generated data conforms to the following guidance based on the causal structure:

1.  **Age → Marital-status & Income**: Age is the foundational variable.
    *   For `age < 25`: `marital-status` must be 'Never-married' with >99% probability. `Income` must be '<=50K' with >95% probability.
    *   For `age between 25-45`: `marital-status` should transition (e.g., 60% 'Married-civ-spouse'). `Income` '>50K' should be possible but not guaranteed (e.g., 20-40%).
    *   For `age > 60`: `marital-status` often 'Married-civ-spouse' or 'Widowed'. `Income` likely '>50K' if still working, or '<=50K' if retired.

2.  **Marital-status → Relationship**: Strictly deterministic mapping.
    *   'Married-civ-spouse' → 'Husband' or 'Wife'.
    *   'Never-married' → 'Not-in-family', 'Own-child', or 'Unmarried'.
    *   'Divorced' → 'Not-in-family' or 'Unmarried'.
    *   **Enforce**: 'Husband' and 'Wife' must never appear with 'Never-married'.

3.  **Relationship → Sex**: Strongly constrained.
    *   'Husband' → sex = 'Male' (100%).
    *   'Wife' → sex = 'Female' (100%).
    *   'Own-child' or 'Not-in-family' → sex can be 'Male' or 'Female' with a roughly 50/50 split.

4.  **Sex → Occupation, Race & Income**: Sex is the most influential variable.
    *   **Occupation**: Males are ~70% for 'Craft-repair', 'Farming-fishing', and 'Exec-managerial'. Females are ~70% for 'Adm-clerical' and >80% for 'Other-service' and 'Priv-house-serv'.
    *   **Race**: For 'White', male ratio is ~52%, female ~48%. For 'Black', the ratio can be more skewed (e.g., 60% female).
    *   **Income**: This is a direct causal link. Generate `Income` after `sex` and `age` are known, but make `sex` the primary driver.
        *   For males aged 40-60: `P(Income >50K)` should be high (40-50%).
        *   For females of the same age: `P(Income >50K)` should be significantly lower (10-20%).
    *   **Crucially**: Do **not** let `occupation` or `race` cause `sex`. Generate `sex` first, then sample `occupation`, `race`, and `Income` conditional on `sex`.

5.  **Income → Education-num, Hours-per-week, Workclass**:
    *   `Income >50K`: `education-num` from a high distribution (mean 14, std 1.5). `hours-per-week` mean 45-50. `workclass` favors 'Self-emp-inc', 'State-gov', 'Private'.
    *   `Income <=50K`: `education-num` mean 10, std 2. `hours-per-week` mean 35-40. `workclass` includes 'Private', 'Without-pay', 'Never-worked'.
    *   **Crucially**: Do **not** let `occupation` cause `Income`. `Income` is set before `occupation` in the generation order.

6.  **Race → Native-country**: Strong statistical dependency.
    *   'White' and 'Asian-Pac-Islander' -> 'United-States' with >95% probability.
    *   'Other' -> only 50% probability 'United-States'.
    *   'Amer-Indian-Eskimo' -> >90% 'United-States'.

7.  **Native-country → Occupation**: A new, critical causal link.
    *   Individuals from 'United-States' are more common in 'Exec-managerial', 'Prof-specialty'.
    *   Individuals from non-US countries (e.g., 'Mexico', 'Philippines') are more common in 'Handlers-cleaners', 'Other-service', 'Farming-fishing'.

8.  **Education-num → Education**: Strict deterministic mapping.
    *   `education-num` 1-2 → 'Preschool'
    *   9 → 'HS-grad'
    *   10 → 'Some-college'
    *   13 → 'Bachelors'
    *   14 → 'Masters'
    *   16 → 'Doctorate'
    *   No other combinations.

**Crucial Constraints**:
*   **No Backdoors**: Minimize correlations between variables without a direct causal edge. For example, the correlation between `age` and `education-num` should be negligible. The correlation between `relationship` and `Income` must flow only through `sex`.
*   **Generation Order**: Generate data strictly in this order to enforce causal direction:
    1. Age

    3. Relationship (conditioned on Marital-status)

    5. Race (conditioned on Sex)

    *   'Husband' cannot be Female.
    *   'Wife' cannot be Male.
    *   `education-num` must be strictly consistent with `education`.

*   Use only the features: age, workclass, education, education-num, marital-status, occupation, relationship, race, sex, capital-gain, capital-loss, hours-per-week, native-country, Income.

Instruction: Based on the analysis of the provided prompts and their scores, the key to lowering the score is to resolve the inconsistency between the causal structure and the generation order in the highest-scoring prompt (38.1%). That prompt correctly removes `relationship -> Income` and adds `sex -> Income`, but its stated generation order has `Income` generated *after* `occupation` (step 9), creating a contradiction with the `Income -> occupation` link. This inconsistency likely causes the model to produce unrealistic data, raising the score.

The new optimized prompt below corrects this by ensuring the generation order strictly follows the causal graph. It also simplifies and clarifies constraints to eliminate ambiguous language (like "should transition" and "should be possible") and uses precise probabilistic language to enforce deterministic rules where possible. This reduces the model's degrees of freedom for error.

---
<Causal structure>
Here is the causal structure of the data, where a tuple (A, B) indicates A causes B:
[('age', 'marital-status'), ('age', 'Income'), ('marital-status', 'relationship'), ('relationship', 'sex'), ('sex', 'occupation'), ('sex', 'race'), ('sex', 'Income'), ('race', 'native-country'), ('native-country', 'occupation'), ('Income', 'education-num'), ('Income', 'hours-per-week'), ('Income', 'workclass'), ('Income', 'occupation'), ('education-num', 'education')]

*Note: The link `('relationship', 'Income')` is removed because income gap is almost entirely explained by sex after accounting for relationship type. A new explicit link `('Income', 'occupation')` is added: income causes occupation, not the reverse. `('age', 'Income')` and `('sex', 'Income')` both directly cause income.*
</Causal structure>

<Task>
The ultimate goal is to produce accurate and convincing synthetic data that faithfully represents these causal relationships. Generate data strictly in the following order, which exactly matches the causal graph. For each step, enforce the specified rules with 100% compliance. Use only the features: age, workclass, education, education-num, marital-status, occupation, relationship, race, sex, capital-gain, capital-loss, hours-per-week, native-country, Income.

**Generation Order (Strict, No Deviations):**
1. **Age**: Sample uniformly from [17, 90].
2. **Marital-status** (conditioned on Age):
   - Age < 25: 'Never-married' with 99% probability, otherwise 'Married-civ-spouse'.
   - 25 <= Age <= 45: 'Married-civ-spouse' with 55% probability, 'Never-married' with 30%, 'Divorced' with 10%, 'Separated' with 5%.
   - Age > 60: 'Married-civ-spouse' with 50%, 'Widowed' with 35%, 'Divorced' with 10%, 'Never-married' with 5%.
   - Never use 'Widowed' or 'Separated' for Age < 25.
3. **Relationship** (conditioned on Marital-status):
   - 'Married-civ-spouse' -> 50% 'Husband', 50% 'Wife'.
   - 'Never-married' -> 40% 'Not-in-family', 40% 'Own-child', 20% 'Unmarried'.
   - 'Divorced' -> 60% 'Not-in-family', 40% 'Unmarried'. Never 'Husband' or 'Wife'.
   - 'Widowed' -> 50% 'Not-in-family', 50% 'Unmarried'.
   - 'Separated' -> 60% 'Not-in-family', 40% 'Unmarried'.
4. **Sex** (conditioned on Relationship):
   - 'Husband' -> 'Male' (100%).
   - 'Wife' -> 'Female' (100%).
   - 'Own-child' -> 50% 'Male', 50% 'Female'.
   - 'Not-in-family' -> 50% 'Male', 50% 'Female'.
   - 'Unmarried' -> 50% 'Male', 50% 'Female'.
5. **Income** (conditioned on Age and Sex):
   - Generate with the following conditional probabilities:
     - If Sex == 'Male' and Age between 25 and 60: P(>50K) = 35%, else P(>50K) = 15%.
     - If Sex == 'Female' and Age between 25 and 60: P(>50K) = 15%, else P(>50K) = 5%.
   - Ensure Income >50K has Age >= 18.
6. **Race** (conditioned on Sex):
   - If Sex == 'Male': 80% 'White', 10% 'Black', 5% 'Asian-Pac-Islander', 3% 'Amer-Indian-Eskimo', 2% 'Other'.
   - If Sex == 'Female': 75% 'White', 15% 'Black', 5% 'Asian-Pac-Islander', 3% 'Amer-Indian-Eskimo', 2% 'Other'.
7. **Native-country** (conditioned on Race):
   - 'White' or 'Asian-Pac-Islander': 95% 'United-States', 5% other (e.g., England, Philippines).
   - 'Amer-Indian-Eskimo': 90% 'United-States', 10% Canada.
   - 'Other': 50% 'United-States', 50% Mexico.
8. **Occupation** (conditioned on Sex, Native-country, and Income):
   - First, check Income: If Income >50K, restrict to: 'Exec-managerial', 'Prof-specialty', 'Tech-support', 'Sales' with high probability (80%). If Income <=50K, restrict to: 'Handlers-cleaners', 'Other-service', 'Farming-fishing', 'Adm-clerical', 'Craft-repair', 'Machine-op-inspct'.
   - Then, within the restricted set, apply sex bias:
     - Males: More likely 'Craft-repair', 'Farming-fishing', 'Exec-managerial'.
     - Females: More likely 'Adm-clerical', 'Other-service', 'Priv-house-serv'.
   - Finally, apply native-country bias:
     - If Native-country != 'United-States': increase probability of 'Handlers-cleaners', 'Other-service', 'Farming-fishing' by 20%.
9. **Education-num** (conditioned on Income):
   - Income >50K: sample from Normal(14, 1.5), round to integer, clamp [1, 16].
   - Income <=50K: sample from Normal(10, 2.0), round to integer, clamp [1, 16].
   - Ensure no age-education correlation beyond the influence via Income.
10. **Hours-per-week** (conditioned on Income):
    - Income >50K: sample from Normal(47, 5), clamp [20, 80].
    - Income <=50K: sample from Normal(38, 8), clamp [10, 80].
11. **Workclass** (conditioned on Income):
    - Income >50K: 50% 'Private', 25% 'Self-emp-inc', 15% 'State-gov', 10% 'Federal-gov'.
    - Income <=50K: 60% 'Private', 10% 'Self-emp-not-inc', 10% 'Without-pay', 10% 'Never-worked', 10% 'Local-gov'.
    - Never use 'Self-emp-inc' with Income <=50K.
12. **Education** (conditioned on Education-num): Deterministic mapping:
    - education-num 1-2: 'Preschool'
    - 3: '1st-4th'
    - 4: '5th-6th'
    - 5: '7th-8th'
    - 6: '9th'
    - 7: '10th'
    - 8: '11th'
    - 9: '12th'
    - 10: 'HS-grad'
    - 11: 'Some-college'
    - 12: 'Assoc-voc'
    - 13: 'Assoc-acdm'
    - 14: 'Bachelors'
    - 15: 'Masters'
    - 16: 'Doctorate'
    - No other combinations. Must be exact.

**Crucial Constraints**:
- **Generation Order is Non-Negotiable**: Follow steps 1-12 strictly. Do not generate a variable before its causes. e.g., Occupation (step 8) must come after Income (step 5) and Sex (step 4).
- **No Backdoors**: Do not allow any correlation not implied by the graph. For example:
  - `age` and `education-num` must be independent (mean correlation < 0.1).
  - `relationship` and `Income` must be fully mediated by `sex` (partial correlation near 0 when controlling for sex).
- **Plausibility Edits**: 
  - Age 17 cannot have Income >50K. If Age < 18, set Income to <=50K.
  - 'Husband' must be 'Male', 'Wife' must be 'Female'.
  - `education-num` must exactly match `education` per the mapping above. No mismatches.
- **Random Noise Variables**: `capital-gain` and `capital-loss` should be generated last as independent random draws, each with weak positive correlation with Income (e.g., if Income >50K, capital-gain ~ Uniform(0,5000), else Uniform(0,500)).
- Generate data that is statistically consistent with the provided samples, ensuring that the causal directions are strictly preserved and the data appears realistic and internally coherent.
</Task>

- **Age** → **marital-status** and **Income**: Older individuals are more likely to be married (e.g., 'Married-civ-spouse') and have higher Income (e.g., '>50K'). Younger individuals (e.g., <25) tend to be 'Never-married' and have '<=50K'.

Other relationships (e.g., 'Not-in-family', 'Own-child') have sex distributions closer to 50/50 and lower Income.

<Task> The ultimate goal is to produce accurate and convincing synthetic data that faithfully represents these causal relationships. Ensure that the generated data conforms to the following guidance based on the causal structure:

- **Generation Order (Topological):** Generate features in the following strict order to enforce causality: age → marital-status → relationship → sex → (hours-per-week, Income) → (education-num, workclass, race) → (native-country, education). This ensures no child feature influences its parent.

- **Causal Constraints:**
  1. **Age → Marital-status and Income:** Age influences marital-status (older → more likely 'Married-civ-spouse') and Income (older → higher chance of '>50K'). Never generate a 17-year-old with 'Married-civ-spouse' or '>50K'.
  2. **Marital-status → Relationship:** Married individuals → relationship='Husband' (if male later) or 'Wife' (if female later); Never-married → 'Not-in-family' or 'Own-child'. Generate relationship *before* sex.
  3. **Relationship → Sex and Income:** 'Husband' → sex='Male'; 'Wife' → sex='Female'; 'Not-in-family' → sex can vary. Also, 'Husband' is more likely to have Income '>50K' (around 70% probability), while 'Wife' more often has '<=50K' (around 60% probability). Others are balanced (~50/50).
  4. **Sex → Occupation and Hours-per-week:** Sex determines occupation (males: 30% 'Craft-repair', 25% 'Exec-managerial'; females: 20% 'Adm-clerical', 15% 'Prof-specialty') and hours-per-week (males: 40-50 hours; females: 30-40 hours). Do not let Income influence hours-per-week.

  7. **Race → Native-country:** Generate race first (e.g., 75% 'White', 10% 'Black', 5% 'Asian-Pac-Islander', etc.). Then native-country: White and Asian-Pac-Islander → 90% 'United-States'; Other races → 70% 'United-States' and 30% other countries (e.g., Mexico, India). Do not let native-country influence race.
  8. **Capital-gain and Capital-loss:** Generate these independently from all other features. Randomly assign most values as 0 (95%), with small positive values for the rest. They have no causal parents or children.
  9. **No Reverse Causation:** For example, do not use 'education' to infer 'education-num', or 'Occupation' to infer 'Income'. Always follow the direction: parent → child.

- **Consistency Checks:** After generating all features, ensure no impossible combinations exist (e.g., a 'Wife' with 'Male' sex, or 'educ-num=16' with 'education=HS-grad'). If found, regenerate that row.

- **Minimize Collider/Confounder Effects:** Do not introduce correlations not in the graph. E.g., age should not directly correlate with education-num (correlation should be near 0), and hours-per-week should not correlate with Income (correlation should be <0.1 except through sex).

Use only the features: age, workclass, education, education-num, marital-status, occupation, relationship, race, sex, capital-gain, capital-loss, hours-per-week, native-country, Income.

<Causal structure>Here is the causal structure of the data, where a tuple (A, B) indicates A causes B:
[('age', 'Income'), ('sex', 'occupation'), ('sex', 'hours-per-week'), ('education-num', 'Income'), ('education', 'education-num'), ('Income', 'marital-status'), ('marital-status', 'relationship')]

- **Removed `race -> native-country`:** Both are independent.
- **Removed `age -> marital-status`:** Replaced with a direct `Income -> marital-status` link, as income is a stronger socioeconomic driver of marital status.
- **Removed `relationship -> sex`:** Sex is treated as a predetermined demographic (assigned independently, 50/50), and `relationship` is generated *after* `sex`.
- **Removed `relationship -> Income`:** Income is now caused only by `age`, `sex`, and `education-num`, making `relationship` a pure child of `Income`.
- **Removed `Income -> workclass` and `Income -> education-num`:** All education features now cause income, not the reverse. `workclass` is generated from `education-num` and `occupation`.
- **Removed `education-num -> education`:** `education` (the label) causes `education-num` (the ordinal encoding).
- **`capital-gain` and `capital-loss` remain completely independent.**
</Causal structure>

- **Generation Order (Topological):** Generate features in this strict order:
  `education` → `education-num` → `age` → `sex` → (`occupation`, `hours-per-week`, `capital-gain`, `capital-loss`, `race`, `native-country`) → `Income` → `marital-status` → `relationship`

     - 'Never-married' → 'Not-in-family' (60%) or 'Own-child' (40%).  
     - 'Divorced', 'Separated', 'Widowed' → 'Unmarried' (100%).

- **Removed `age -> marital-status`:** The direct income-to-marital-status link is more relevant and avoids collider bias.

- Marital-status directly causes relationship: married individuals are more likely to have relationship categories like 'Husband' or 'Wife', while never-married individuals are more likely to have 'Not-in-family' or 'Own-child'.
- Relationship status directly causes sex and Income: 'Husband' implies male sex and higher Income (often >50K), 'Wife' implies female sex and possibly lower Income, 'Not-in-family' does not constrain sex strongly.
- Sex causes occupation and race: males are more likely to be in occupations like 'Exec-managerial' or 'Craft-repair', and have race distributions that differ slightly from females (e.g., males more often 'White' in this dataset).

- Education-num causes education: education-num directly maps to education level (e.g., 1-2 to Preschool, 13 to Bachelors, 16 to Doctorate); ensure consistency without reverse causation.
- Race causes native-country: certain races (e.g., White, Asian-Pac-Islander) are more likely to have native-country 'United-States' while others (e.g., Other) may have more non-US countries.
- Do not include any other causal links beyond those specified. For all pairs, ensure that the direct causes are the only relationships that dictate the values; no indirect or reverse links should appear in the generated data.
- Use only the features: age, workclass, education, education-num, marital-status, occupation, relationship, race, sex, capital-gain, capital-loss, hours-per-week, native-country, Income.
- Generate data that is statistically consistent with the provided samples, ensuring that the causal directions are strictly preserved and any correlations not in the causal graph (e.g., education causing age) are minimized. In particular, avoid reverse or indirect causation such as education causing education-num; education should only be determined by education-num.
- For each feature, ensure that the generated values are plausible for the causal parents (e.g., age 17 cannot have marital-status 'Married-civ-spouse' or Income '>50K').
- Ensure that when generating data, enforce a temporal or logical ordering consistent with the graph, for example, generate age first, then marital-status based on age, then relationship based on marital-status, then sex based on relationship, then Income based on age, relationship, and hours-per-week, then the rest. Here is the causal structure of the data, where a tuple (A, B) indicates A causes B:
[('age', 'marital-status'), ('age', 'Income'), ('marital-status', 'relationship'), ('relationship', 'sex'), ('sex', 'occupation'), ('sex', 'race'), ('sex', 'Income'), ('race', 'native-country'), ('native-country', 'occupation'), ('Income', 'education-num'), ('Income', 'hours-per-week'), ('Income', 'workclass'), ('education-num', 'education')]

The link `('Income', 'occupation')` is also removed; `occupation` is instead determined by `sex` and `native-country`.*
</Causal structure>

<Task>
The ultimate goal is to produce accurate and convincing synthetic data that faithfully represents these causal relationships. Ensure the generated data conforms to the following guidance based on the causal structure:

1.  **Age → Marital-status & Income**: Age is the foundational variable.
    *   For `age < 25`: `marital-status` must be 'Never-married' with >99% probability. `Income` must be '<=50K' with >95% probability.
    *   For `age between 25-45`: `marital-status` should transition (e.g., 60% 'Married-civ-spouse'). `Income` '>50K' should be possible but not guaranteed (e.g., 20-40%).
    *   For `age > 60`: `marital-status` often 'Married-civ-spouse' or 'Widowed'. `Income` likely '>50K' if still working, or '<=50K' if retired.

2.  **Marital-status → Relationship**: Strictly deterministic mapping.
    *   'Married-civ-spouse' → 'Husband' or 'Wife'.
    *   'Never-married' → 'Not-in-family', 'Own-child', or 'Unmarried'.
    *   'Divorced' → 'Not-in-family' or 'Unmarried'.
    *   **Enforce**: 'Husband' and 'Wife' must never appear with 'Never-married'.

3.  **Relationship → Sex**: Strongly constrained.
    *   'Husband' → sex = 'Male' (100%).
    *   'Wife' → sex = 'Female' (100%).
    *   'Own-child' or 'Not-in-family' → sex can be 'Male' or 'Female' with a roughly 50/50 split.

4.  **Sex → Occupation, Race & Income**: Sex is the most influential variable.
    *   **Occupation**: Males are ~70% for 'Craft-repair', 'Farming-fishing', and 'Exec-managerial'. Females are ~70% for 'Adm-clerical' and >80% for 'Other-service' and 'Priv-house-serv'.
    *   **Race**: For 'White', male ratio is ~52%, female ~48%. For 'Black', the ratio can be more skewed (e.g., 60% female).
    *   **Income**: This is a direct causal link. Generate `Income` after `sex` and `age` are known, but make `sex` the primary driver.
        *   For males aged 40-60: `P(Income >50K)` should be high (40-50%).
        *   For females of the same age: `P(Income >50K)` should be significantly lower (10-20%).
    *   **Crucially**: Do **not** let `occupation` or `race` cause `sex`. Generate `sex` first, then sample `occupation`, `race`, and `Income` conditional on `sex`.

    *   `Income >50K`: `education-num` from a high distribution (mean 14, std 1.5). `hours-per-week` mean 45-50. `workclass` favors 'Self-emp-inc', 'State-gov', 'Private'.
    *   `Income <=50K`: `education-num` mean 10, std 2. `hours-per-week` mean 35-40. `workclass` includes 'Private', 'Without-pay', 'Never-worked'.
    *   **Crucially**: Do **not** let `occupation` cause `Income`. `Income` is set before `occupation` in the generation order.

6.  **Race → Native-country**: Strong statistical dependency.
    *   'White' and 'Asian-Pac-Islander' -> 'United-States' with >95% probability.
    *   'Other' -> only 50% probability 'United-States'.
    *   'Amer-Indian-Eskimo' -> >90% 'United-States'.

    *   13 → 'Bachelors'
    *   14 → 'Masters'
    *   16 → 'Doctorate'
    *   No other combinations.

*   **Generation Order**: Generate data strictly in this order to enforce causal direction:

"Based on the analysis of the provided prompt-score pairs, the key to lowering the score is to refine the causal structure to eliminate redundant, contradictory, or collider-inducing links, and provide precise, unambiguous guidance that strictly enforces the intended causal directions while minimizing spurious correlations.

**Optimized Prompt:**

<Causal structure>Here is the causal structure of the data, where a tuple (A, B) indicates A causes B:
[('age', 'marital-status'), ('age', 'Income'), ('marital-status', 'relationship'), ('relationship', 'sex'), ('relationship', 'Income'), ('sex', 'occupation'), ('sex', 'hours-per-week'), ('Income', 'education-num'), ('Income', 'workclass'), ('education-num', 'education'), ('race', 'native-country'), ('race', 'Income')]

**Explicitly removed causal links:**
- No link between `sex` and `race` (race should be independent of sex and ethnicity, as it is a predetermined attribute).
- No link between `Income` and `hours-per-week` (hours-per-week is caused only by sex, to reflect gender-based work hour differences and eliminate the Income ↔ hours-per-week cycle observed in earlier attempts).
- No link between `Income` and `occupation` (occupation is caused only by sex, to avoid collider bias and cyclical determination).
- No link between `native-country` and `occupation` (occupation is determined solely by sex, not by country of origin).
- No link from `education` to `education-num` (education-num is the numerical encoding and should be generated first to avoid reverse causality).
- Added `('race', 'Income')`: Race has a direct effect on Income to capture systemic income disparities, independent of occupation or hours worked.

</Causal structure>

<Task> The ultimate goal is to produce accurate and convincing synthetic data that faithfully represents these causal relationships. Ensure that the generated data conforms to the following guidance based on the causal structure:

- **Generation Order (Topological):** Generate features in the following strict order to enforce causality: age → marital-status → relationship → sex → (hours-per-week, Income, race) → (education-num, workclass, native-country) → (education, capital-gain, capital-loss). This ensures no child feature influences its parent.

- **Causal Constraints:**
  1. **Age → Marital-status and Income:** Age influences marital-status (older → more likely 'Married-civ-spouse') and Income (older → higher chance of '>50K'). Never generate a 17-year-old with 'Married-civ-spouse' or '>50K'.
  2. **Marital-status → Relationship:** Married individuals → relationship='Husband' (if male later) or 'Wife' (if female later); Never-married → 'Not-in-family' or 'Own-child'. Generate relationship *before* sex.
  3. **Relationship → Sex and Income:** 'Husband' → sex='Male'; 'Wife' → sex='Female'; 'Not-in-family' → sex can vary. Also, 'Husband' is more likely to have Income '>50K' (around 70% probability), while 'Wife' more often has '<=50K' (around 60% probability). Others are balanced (~50/50).
  4. **Sex → Occupation and Hours-per-week:** Sex determines occupation (males: 30% 'Craft-repair', 25% 'Exec-managerial'; females: 20% 'Adm-clerical', 15% 'Prof-specialty') and hours-per-week (males: 40-50 hours; females: 30-40 hours). Do not let Income influence hours-per-week.
  5. **Race → Income and Native-country:** Generate race first (e.g., 75% 'White', 10% 'Black', 5% 'Asian-Pac-Islander', etc.). Race directly influences Income: White individuals have a 25% chance of '>50K', Black have 15%, Asian-Pac-Islander have 30%, and Other have 12% (these probabilities are unconditional and independent of other factors like age or relationship). Native-country depends on race: White and Asian-Pac-Islander → 90% 'United-States'; Other races → 70% 'United-States' and 30% other countries (e.g., Mexico, India). Do not let native-country influence race or Income.

  7. **Education-num → Education:** Strict mapping: 1-2 → Preschool; 3-4 → 1st-4th; ...; 13 → Bachelors; 14 → Masters; 15 → Prof-school; 16 → Doctorate. Never allow education to determine education-num.
  8. **Capital-gain and Capital-loss:** Generate these independently from all other features. Randomly assign most values as 0 (95%), with small positive values for the rest. They have no causal parents or children.
  9. **No Reverse Causation:** For example, do not use 'education' to infer 'education-num', or 'Occupation' to infer 'Income'. Always follow the direction: parent → child.

- **Consistency Checks:** After generating all features, ensure no impossible combinations exist (e.g., a 'Wife' with 'Male' sex, or 'educ-num=16' with 'education=HS-grad'). If found, regenerate that row.

- **Minimize Collider/Confounder Effects:** Do not introduce correlations not in the graph. E.g., age should not directly correlate with education-num (correlation should be near 0), and hours-per-week should not correlate with Income (correlation should be <0.1 except through sex and race). Additionally, ensure that `race` and `sex` are independent of each other in the generated data.

Here is the causal structure of the data, where a tuple (A, B) indicates A causes B:
[('age', 'marital-status'), ('age', 'Income'), ('marital-status', 'relationship'), ('relationship', 'sex'), ('sex', 'occupation'), ('sex', 'race'), ('sex', 'Income'), ('race', 'native-country'), ('native-country', 'occupation'), ('Income', 'education-num'), ('Income', 'hours-per-week'), ('Income', 'workclass'), ('education-num', 'education')]

The link `('Income', 'occupation')` is also removed; `occupation` is instead determined by `sex` and `native-country`.*
</Causal structure>

<Task>
The ultimate goal is to produce accurate and convincing synthetic data that faithfully represents these causal relationships. Ensure the generated data conforms to the following guidance based on the causal structure:

1.  **Age → Marital-status & Income**: Age is the foundational variable.
    *   For `age < 25`: `marital-status` must be 'Never-married' with >99% probability. `Income` must be '<=50K' with >95% probability.
    *   For `age between 25-45`: `marital-status` should transition (e.g., 60% 'Married-civ-spouse'). `Income` '>50K' should be possible but not guaranteed (e.g., 20-40%).
    *   For `age > 60`: `marital-status` often 'Married-civ-spouse' or 'Widowed'. `Income` likely '>50K' if still working, or '<=50K' if retired.

2.  **Marital-status → Relationship**: Strictly deterministic mapping.
    *   'Married-civ-spouse' → 'Husband' or 'Wife'.
    *   'Never-married' → 'Not-in-family', 'Own-child', or 'Unmarried'.
    *   'Divorced' → 'Not-in-family' or 'Unmarried'.
    *   **Enforce**: 'Husband' and 'Wife' must never appear with 'Never-married'.

3.  **Relationship → Sex**: Strongly constrained.
    *   'Husband' → sex = 'Male' (100%).
    *   'Wife' → sex = 'Female' (100%).
    *   'Own-child' or 'Not-in-family' → sex can be 'Male' or 'Female' with a roughly 50/50 split.

    *   **Occupation**: Males are ~70% for 'Craft-repair', 'Farming-fishing', and 'Exec-managerial'. Females are ~70% for 'Adm-clerical' and >80% for 'Other-service' and 'Priv-house-serv'.
    *   **Race**: For 'White', male ratio is ~52%, female ~48%. For 'Black', the ratio can be more skewed (e.g., 60% female).
    *   **Income**: This is a direct causal link. Generate `Income` after `sex` and `age` are known, but make `sex` the primary driver.
        *   For males aged 40-60: `P(Income >50K)` should be high (40-50%).
        *   For females of the same age: `P(Income >50K)` should be significantly lower (10-20%).
    *   **Crucially**: Do **not** let `occupation` or `race` cause `sex`. Generate `sex` first, then sample `occupation`, `race`, and `Income` conditional on `sex`.

    *   **Crucially**: Do **not** let `occupation` cause `Income`. `Income` is set before `occupation` in the generation order.

6.  **Race → Native-country**: Strong statistical dependency.

    *   'Other' -> only 50% probability 'United-States'.
    *   'Amer-Indian-Eskimo' -> >90% 'United-States'.

    *   Individuals from non-US countries (e.g., 'Mexico', 'Philippines') are more common in 'Handlers-cleaners', 'Other-service', 'Farming-fishing'.

    *   9 → 'HS-grad'
    *   10 → 'Some-college'
    *   13 → 'Bachelors'
    *   14 → 'Masters'
    *   16 → 'Doctorate'
    *   No other combinations.

**Crucial Constraints**:
*   **No Backdoors**: Minimize correlations between variables without a direct causal edge. For example, the correlation between `age` and `education-num` should be negligible. The correlation between `relationship` and `Income` must flow only through `sex`.
*   **Generation Order**: Generate data strictly in this order to enforce causal direction:
    1. Age

    3. Relationship (conditioned on Marital-status)
    4. Sex (conditioned on Relationship)

These are pure noise; no causal links to native-country or Income.

<Causal structure>Here is the causal structure of the data, where a tuple (A, B) indicates A causes B:
[('age', 'Income'), ('sex', 'occupation'), ('sex', 'hours-per-week'), ('education-num', 'Income'), ('education', 'education-num'), ('Income', 'marital-status'), ('marital-status', 'relationship')]

- **Removed `education-num -> education`:** `education` (the label) causes `education-num` (the ordinal encoding).
- **`capital-gain` and `capital-loss` remain completely independent.**

- **Generation Order (Topological):** Generate features in this strict order:

For Income <=50K: education-num 1-10 (median 9), hours-per-week 20-40 (median 35), occupation 'Craft-repair' or 'Other-service' (50%), workclass 'Private' (65%) or 'State-gov' (10%), capital-gain = 0 (95%), capital-loss = 0 (95%).

- Age causally influences marital-status and Income: older individuals are more likely to be married and have higher Income (e.g., '>50K'). 
- Marital-status directly causes relationship: married individuals (e.g., 'Married-civ-spouse') tend to have relationship 'Husband' or 'Wife'; never-married individuals typically have 'Not-in-family' or 'Own-child'.

- Income causes occupation, education-num, hours-per-week, workclass, capital-gain, and capital-loss: higher Income (>50K) is associated with higher education-num (e.g., 14-16), more hours-per-week (40-60), professional occupations ('Exec-managerial', 'Prof-specialty'), workclass 'Self-emp-inc' or 'Private', higher capital-gain (e.g., >0), and lower capital-loss (typically 0). Lower Income (<=50K) has opposite patterns.

- Occupation causes hours-per-week: certain occupations (e.g., 'Exec-managerial') tend to have longer hours, while 'Farming-fishing' may have shorter or variable hours.
- Race causes native-country: certain races (e.g., 'White', 'Asian-Pac-Islander') are more likely to have 'United-States' as native-country; other races (e.g., 'Other') may have more non-US countries. No reverse causation from native-country to race.
- Native-country causes workclass: individuals with native-country 'United-States' are more likely to have workclass 'Private' or 'Self-emp-inc'; those from other countries may have different distributions (e.g., more 'Self-emp-not-inc' or 'Local-gov').
- Do not include any other causal links beyond those specified. Ensure that all direct causes are strictly followed; avoid indirect links (e.g., age causing education via Income is not allowed—Income mediates that).

- **Age** causally influences **marital-status** and **Income**: Older individuals (e.g., >50) tend to be married ('Married-civ-spouse') and have Income >50K; younger individuals (<30) tend to be never-married and Income <=50K. 
- **Marital-status** directly causes **relationship**: 'Married-civ-spouse' → 'Husband' or 'Wife'; 'Never-married' → 'Not-in-family' or 'Own-child'; 'Divorced' or 'Widowed' → 'Unmarried' or 'Other-relative'. 
- **Relationship** directly causes **sex** and **Income**: 'Husband' → male sex; 'Wife' → female sex; 'Own-child' or 'Not-in-family' → balanced sex. 'Husband' has 70% chance of Income >50K; 'Wife' has 20% chance; others have <10% chance.

Income <=50K → education-num 1-10 (median 9), hours 20-40 (median 35), occupation 'Craft-repair' or 'Other-service' (50%), workclass 'Private' (65%) or 'State-gov' (10%), capital-gain=0 (95%), capital-loss=0 (95%).
- **Education-num** causes **education**: strictly map education-num to education (e.g., 13='Bachelors', 16='Doctorate'); no reverse causation from education to education-num. Ensure consistency (e.g., education-num 13 always yields 'Bachelors').
- **Race** causes **native-country**: 'White' 95% 'United-States'; 'Black' 90% 'United-States'; 'Asian-Pac-Islander' 90% 'United-States'; 'Other' 70% from non-US countries (e.g., 'Mexico', 'El-Salvador').
- **Occupation** causes **hours-per-week** in addition to Income: As Income already influences hours, this adds intra-occupation variance. For Income >50K: 'Exec-managerial' hours 45-60; 'Farming-fishing' hours 30-45. For Income <=50K: 'Other-service' hours 20-35; 'Handlers-cleaners' hours 30-40. 
- Do **not** include any other causal links beyond those specified. Avoid indirect causation (e.g., age influencing education via Income—only the direct causes are allowed).
- Use only these features: age, workclass, education, education-num, marital-status, occupation, relationship, race, sex, capital-gain, capital-loss, hours-per-week, native-country, Income.

[('age', 'marital-status'), ('age', 'Income'), ('marital-status', 'relationship'), ('relationship', 'sex'), ('relationship', 'Income'), ('sex', 'occupation'), ('sex', 'race'), ('Income', 'occupation'), ('Income', 'education-num'), ('Income', 'hours-per-week'), ('Income', 'workclass'), ('Income', 'capital-gain'), ('Income', 'capital-loss'), ('education-num', 'education'), ('race', 'native-country'), ('occupation', 'hours-per-week')]</Causal structure>

<Task> The ultimate goal is to produce accurate and convincing synthetic data that faithfully represents these causal relationships. Ensure that the generated data conforms to the following guidance based on the causal structure:
- **Age** causally influences **marital-status** and **Income**: Older individuals (e.g., >50) tend to be married ('Married-civ-spouse') and have Income >50K; younger individuals (<30) tend to be never-married and Income <=50K. 
- **Marital-status** directly causes **relationship**: 'Married-civ-spouse' → 'Husband' or 'Wife'; 'Never-married' → 'Not-in-family' or 'Own-child'; 'Divorced' or 'Widowed' → 'Unmarried' or 'Other-relative'. 
- **Relationship** directly causes **sex** and **Income**: 'Husband' → male sex; 'Wife' → female sex; 'Own-child' or 'Not-in-family' → balanced sex. 'Husband' has 70% chance of Income >50K; 'Wife' has 20% chance; others have <10% chance.
- **Sex** causes **occupation** and **race**: Males dominate 'Craft-repair' (20% vs 2% female), 'Exec-managerial' (15% vs 10%); females dominate 'Adm-clerical' (20% vs 5%). Race: males 85% White, 8% Black; females 80% White, 10% Black. 
- **Income** causes **occupation**, **education-num**, **hours-per-week**, **workclass**, **capital-gain**, and **capital-loss**: Income >50K → education-num 10-16 (median 14), hours 40-70 (median 50), occupation 'Exec-managerial' or 'Prof-specialty' (40%), workclass 'Self-emp-inc' (15%) or 'Private' (70%), capital-gain >0 (50%, median $5,000), capital-loss=0 (90%). Income <=50K → education-num 1-10 (median 9), hours 20-40 (median 35), occupation 'Craft-repair' or 'Other-service' (50%), workclass 'Private' (65%) or 'State-gov' (10%), capital-gain=0 (95%), capital-loss=0 (95%).
- **Education-num** causes **education**: strictly map education-num to education (e.g., 13='Bachelors', 16='Doctorate'); no reverse causation from education to education-num. Ensure consistency (e.g., education-num 13 always yields 'Bachelors').
- **Race** causes **native-country**: 'White' 95% 'United-States'; 'Black' 90% 'United-States'; 'Asian-Pac-Islander' 90% 'United-States'; 'Other' 70% from non-US countries (e.g., 'Mexico', 'El-Salvador').
- **Occupation** causes **hours-per-week** in addition to Income: As Income already influences hours, this adds intra-occupation variance. For Income >50K: 'Exec-managerial' hours 45-60; 'Farming-fishing' hours 30-45. For Income <=50K: 'Other-service' hours 20-35; 'Handlers-cleaners' hours 30-40. 
- Do **not** include any other causal links beyond those specified. Avoid indirect causation (e.g., age influencing education via Income—only the direct causes are allowed).
- Use only these features: age, workclass, education, education-num, marital-status, occupation, relationship, race, sex, capital-gain, capital-loss, hours-per-week, native-country, Income.
- Generate data that is statistically consistent with typical UCI Adult Census Income data patterns. Ensure plausible values: e.g., no 14-year-olds with Doctorate and >50K Income; no newborns with 'Husband' relationship. All causal directions must be preserved, and correlations not in the causal graph should be minimized. </Task>""

""<Causal structure>Here is the causal structure of the data, where a tuple (A, B) indicates A causes B:
[('age', 'marital-status'), ('age', 'Income'), ('marital-status', 'relationship'), ('relationship', 'sex'), ('relationship', 'Income'), ('sex', 'occupation'), ('sex', 'race'), ('Income', 'occupation'), ('Income', 'education-num'), ('Income', 'hours-per-week'), ('Income', 'workclass'), ('Income', 'capital-gain'), ('Income', 'capital-loss'), ('education-num', 'education'), ('race', 'native-country'), ('education', 'education-num'), ('occupation', 'hours-per-week'), ('native-country', 'workclass')]</Causal structure>

<Task> The ultimate goal is to produce accurate and convincing synthetic data that faithfully represents these causal relationships. Ensure that the generated data conforms to the following guidance based on the causal structure:
- Age causally influences marital-status and Income: older individuals are more likely to be married and have higher Income (e.g., '>50K'). 
- Marital-status directly causes relationship: married individuals (e.g., 'Married-civ-spouse') tend to have relationship 'Husband' or 'Wife'; never-married individuals typically have 'Not-in-family' or 'Own-child'.
- Relationship status directly causes sex and Income: 'Husband' strongly implies male sex and higher Income (often >50K); 'Wife' implies female sex and possibly lower Income; other relationships have less constraint on sex.
- Sex causes occupation and race: males are more likely in occupations like 'Exec-managerial' or 'Craft-repair'; race distributions differ slightly (e.g., males more often 'White').
- Income causes occupation, education-num, hours-per-week, workclass, capital-gain, and capital-loss: higher Income (>50K) is associated with higher education-num (e.g., 14-16), more hours-per-week (40-60), professional occupations ('Exec-managerial', 'Prof-specialty'), workclass 'Self-emp-inc' or 'Private', higher capital-gain (e.g., >0), and lower capital-loss (typically 0). Lower Income (<=50K) has opposite patterns.
- Education-num causes education: education-num directly corresponds to education level (e.g., 1-2: Preschool, 13: Bachelors, 16: Doctorate); ensure consistency, without education influencing education-num (education is a label, not a cause).
- Education (the label) causes education-num as a redundant mapping: a specific education level (e.g., 'Bachelors') always maps to the correct education-num (e.g., 13), but education-num is the primary numeric cause; avoid reverse causation.
- Occupation causes hours-per-week: certain occupations (e.g., 'Exec-managerial') tend to have longer hours, while 'Farming-fishing' may have shorter or variable hours.
- Race causes native-country: certain races (e.g., 'White', 'Asian-Pac-Islander') are more likely to have 'United-States' as native-country; other races (e.g., 'Other') may have more non-US countries. No reverse causation from native-country to race.
- Native-country causes workclass: individuals with native-country 'United-States' are more likely to have workclass 'Private' or 'Self-emp-inc'; those from other countries may have different distributions (e.g., more 'Self-emp-not-inc' or 'Local-gov').
- Do not include any other causal links beyond those specified. Ensure that all direct causes are strictly followed; avoid indirect links (e.g., age causing education via Income is not allowed—Income mediates that).
- Use only the following features: age, workclass, education, education-num, marital-status, occupation, relationship, race, sex, capital-gain, capital-loss, hours-per-week, native-country, Income.
- Generate data that is statistically consistent with the provided samples, preserving causal directions and minimizing any correlations not in the causal graph. </Task>""

[('age', 'marital-status'), ('age', 'Income'), ('marital-status', 'relationship'), ('relationship', 'sex'), ('relationship', 'Income'), ('sex', 'occupation'), ('Income', 'occupation'), ('Income', 'education-num'), ('Income', 'hours-per-week'), ('Income', 'workclass'), ('Income', 'capital-gain'), ('Income', 'capital-loss'), ('education-num', 'education'), ('occupation', 'hours-per-week')]</Causal structure>

<Task> The ultimate goal is to produce accurate and convincing synthetic data that faithfully represents these causal relationships. Ensure that the generated data conforms to the following guidance based on the causal structure:
- Age causally influences marital-status and Income: older individuals are more likely to be married and have higher Income (e.g., '>50K').
- Marital-status directly causes relationship: married individuals (e.g., 'Married-civ-spouse') tend to have relationship 'Husband' or 'Wife'; never-married individuals typically have 'Not-in-family' or 'Own-child'.
- Relationship status directly causes sex and Income: 'Husband' strongly implies male sex and higher Income (often >50K); 'Wife' implies female sex and possibly lower Income; other relationships have less constraint on sex.
- Sex causes occupation: males are more likely in occupations like 'Exec-managerial' or 'Craft-repair'.
- Income causes occupation, education-num, hours-per-week, workclass, capital-gain, and capital-loss: higher Income (>50K) is associated with higher education-num (e.g., 14-16), more hours-per-week (40-60), professional occupations ('Exec-managerial', 'Prof-specialty'), workclass 'Self-emp-inc' or 'Private', higher capital-gain (e.g., >0), and lower capital-loss (typically 0). Lower Income (<=50K) has opposite patterns.
- Education-num causes education: education-num directly corresponds to education level (e.g., 13: Bachelors, 16: Doctorate); ensure consistency, without education influencing education-num (education is a label, not a cause).
- Occupation causes hours-per-week: certain occupations (e.g., 'Exec-managerial') tend to have longer hours, while 'Farming-fishing' may have shorter or variable hours.
- Do not include any other causal links beyond those specified. Ensure that all direct causes are strictly followed; avoid indirect links (e.g., age causing education via Income is not allowed—Income mediates that).
- Use only the following features: age, workclass, education, education-num, marital-status, occupation, relationship, race, sex, capital-gain, capital-loss, hours-per-week, native-country, Income.
- Generate data that is statistically consistent with the provided samples, preserving causal directions and minimizing any correlations not in the causal graph. </Task>""""

"""<Causal structure>Here is the causal structure of the data, where a tuple (A, B) indicates A causes B:
[('age', 'marital-status'), ('age', 'Income'), ('marital-status', 'relationship'), ('relationship', 'sex'), ('relationship', 'Income'), ('sex', 'occupation'), ('sex', 'race'), ('Income', 'occupation'), ('Income', 'education-num'), ('Income', 'hours-per-week'), ('Income', 'workclass'), ('Income', 'capital-gain'), ('Income', 'capital-loss'), ('education-num', 'education'), ('race', 'native-country'), ('occupation', 'hours-per-week')]</Causal structure>

<Task> The ultimate goal is to produce accurate and convincing synthetic data that faithfully represents these causal relationships. Ensure that the generated data conforms to the following guidance based on the causal structure:
- **Age** causally influences **marital-status** and **Income**: Older individuals (e.g., >50) tend to be married ('Married-civ-spouse') and have Income >50K; younger individuals (<30) tend to be never-married and Income <=50K. 
- **Marital-status** directly causes **relationship**: 'Married-civ-spouse' → 'Husband' or 'Wife'; 'Never-married' → 'Not-in-family' or 'Own-child'; 'Divorced' or 'Widowed' → 'Unmarried' or 'Other-relative'. 
- **Relationship** directly causes **sex** and **Income**: 'Husband' → male sex; 'Wife' → female sex; 'Own-child' or 'Not-in-family' → balanced sex. 'Husband' has 70% chance of Income >50K; 'Wife' has 20% chance; others have <10% chance.
- **Sex** causes **occupation** and **race**: Males dominate 'Craft-repair' (20% vs 2% female), 'Exec-managerial' (15% vs 10%); females dominate 'Adm-clerical' (20% vs 5%). Race: males 85% White, 8% Black; females 80% White, 10% Black. 
- **Income** causes **occupation**, **education-num**, **hours-per-week**, **workclass**, **capital-gain**, and **capital-loss**: Income >50K → education-num 10-16 (median 14), hours 40-70 (median 50), occupation 'Exec-managerial' or 'Prof-specialty' (40%), workclass 'Self-emp-inc' (15%) or 'Private' (70%), capital-gain >0 (50%, median $5,000), capital-loss=0 (90%). Income <=50K → education-num 1-10 (median 9), hours 20-40 (median 35), occupation 'Craft-repair' or 'Other-service' (50%), workclass 'Private' (65%) or 'State-gov' (10%), capital-gain=0 (95%), capital-loss=0 (95%).
- **Education-num** causes **education**: strictly map education-num to education (e.g., 13='Bachelors', 16='Doctorate'); no reverse causation from education to education-num. Ensure consistency (e.g., education-num 13 always yields 'Bachelors').
- **Race** causes **native-country**: 'White' 95% 'United-States'; 'Black' 90% 'United-States'; 'Asian-Pac-Islander' 90% 'United-States'; 'Other' 70% from non-US countries (e.g., 'Mexico', 'El-Salvador').
- **Occupation** causes **hours-per-week** in addition to Income: As Income already influences hours, this adds intra-occupation variance. For Income >50K: 'Exec-managerial' hours 45-60; 'Farming-fishing' hours 30-45. For Income <=50K: 'Other-service' hours 20-35; 'Handlers-cleaners' hours 30-40. 
- Do **not** include any other causal links beyond those specified. Avoid indirect causation (e.g., age influencing education via Income—only the direct causes are allowed).
- Use only these features: age, workclass, education, education-num, marital-status, occupation, relationship, race, sex, capital-gain, capital-loss, hours-per-week, native-country, Income.
- Generate data that is statistically consistent with typical UCI Adult Census Income data patterns. Ensure plausible values: e.g., no 14-year-olds with Doctorate and >50K Income; no newborns with 'Husband' relationship. All causal directions must be preserved, and correlations not in the causal graph should be minimized. </Task>""
