- Marital-status directly causes relationship: married individuals (e.g., 'Married-civ-spouse') tend to have relationship 'Husband' or 'Wife'; never-married individuals typically have 'Not-in-family' or 'Own-child'.
- Relationship status directly causes sex and Income: 'Husband' strongly implies male sex and higher Income (often >50K); 'Wife' implies female sex and possibly lower Income; other relationships have less constraint on sex.
- Sex causes occupation and race: males are more likely in occupations like 'Exec-managerial' or 'Craft-repair'; race distributions differ slightly (e.g., males more often 'White').
- Income causes occupation, education-num, hours-per-week, workclass, capital-gain, and capital-loss: higher Income (>50K) is associated with higher education-num (e.g., 14-16), more hours-per-week (40-60), professional occupations ('Exec-managerial', 'Prof-specialty'), workclass 'Self-emp-inc' or 'Private', higher capital-gain (e.g., >0), and lower capital-loss (typically 0). Lower Income (<=50K) has opposite patterns.
- Education-num causes education: education-num directly corresponds to education level (e.g., 1-2: Preschool, 13: Bachelors, 16: Doctorate); ensure consistency, without education influencing education-num (education is a label, not a cause).
- Education (the label) causes education-num as a redundant mapping: a specific education level (e.g., 'Bachelors') always maps to the correct education-num (e.g., 13), but education-num is the primary numeric cause; avoid reverse causation.
- Occupation causes hours-per-week: certain occupations (e.g., 'Exec-managerial') tend to have longer hours, while 'Farming-fishing' may have shorter or variable hours.
- Race causes native-country: certain races (e.g., 'White', 'Asian-Pac-Islander') are more likely to have 'United-States' as native-country; other races (e.g., 'Other') may have more non-US countries. No reverse causation from native-country to race.
- Native-country causes workclass: individuals with native-country 'United-States' are more likely to have workclass 'Private' or 'Self-emp-inc'; those from other countries may have different distributions (e.g., more 'Self-emp-not-inc' or 'Local-gov').
- Do not include any other causal links beyond those specified. Ensure that all direct causes are strictly followed; avoid indirect links (e.g., age causing education via Income is not allowed—Income mediates that).
- Use only the following features: age, workclass, education, education-num, marital-status, occupation, relationship, race, sex, capital-gain, capital-loss, hours-per-week, native-country, Income.
- Generate data that is statistically consistent with the provided samples, preserving causal directions and minimizing any correlations not in the causal graph. </Task>""

<Task> The ultimate goal is to produce accurate and convincing synthetic data that faithfully represents these causal relationships. Ensure that the generated data conforms to the following guidance based on the causal structure:
- **Age** causally influences **marital-status** and **Income**: Older individuals (e.g., >50) tend to be married ('Married-civ-spouse') and have Income >50K; younger individuals (<30) tend to be never-married and Income <=50K. 
- **Marital-status** directly causes **relationship**: 'Married-civ-spouse' → 'Husband' or 'Wife'; 'Never-married' → 'Not-in-family' or 'Own-child'; 'Divorced' or 'Widowed' → 'Unmarried' or 'Other-relative'. 
- **Relationship** directly causes **sex** and **Income**: 'Husband' → male sex; 'Wife' → female sex; 'Own-child' or 'Not-in-family' → balanced sex. 'Husband' has 70% chance of Income >50K; 'Wife' has 20% chance; others have <10% chance.
- **Sex** causes **occupation** and **race**: Males dominate 'Craft-repair' (20% vs 2% female), 'Exec-managerial' (15% vs 10%); females dominate 'Adm-clerical' (20% vs 5%). Race: males 85% White, 8% Black; females 80% White, 10% Black. 
- **Income** causes **occupation**, **education-num**, **hours-per-week**, **workclass**, **capital-gain**, and **capital-loss**: Income >50K → education-num 10-16 (median 14), hours 40-70 (median 50), occupation 'Exec-managerial' or 'Prof-specialty' (40%), workclass 'Self-emp-inc' (15%) or 'Private' (70%), capital-gain >0 (50%, median $5,000), capital-loss=0 (90%). Income <=50K → education-num 1-10 (median 9), hours 20-40 (median 35), occupation 'Craft-repair' or 'Other-service' (50%), workclass 'Private' (65%) or 'State-gov' (10%), capital-gain=0 (95%), capital-loss=0 (95%).
- **Education-num** causes **education**: strictly map education-num to education (e.g., 13='Bachelors', 16='Doctorate'); no reverse causation from education to education-num. Ensure consistency (e.g., education-num 13 always yields 'Bachelors').
- **Race** causes **native-country**: 'White' 95% 'United-States'; 'Black' 90% 'United-States'; 'Asian-Pac-Islander' 90% 'United-States'; 'Other' 70% from non-US countries (e.g., 'Mexico', 'El-Salvador').
- **Occupation** causes **hours-per-week** in addition to Income: As Income already influences hours, this adds intra-occupation variance. For Income >50K: 'Exec-managerial' hours 45-60; 'Farming-fishing' hours 30-45. For Income <=50K: 'Other-service' hours 20-35; 'Handlers-cleaners' hours 30-40. 
- Do **not** include any other causal links beyond those specified. Avoid indirect causation (e.g., age influencing education via Income—only the direct causes are allowed).
- Use only these features: age, workclass, education, education-num, marital-status, occupation, relationship, race, sex, capital-gain, capital-loss, hours-per-week, native-country, Income.
- Generate data that is statistically consistent with typical UCI Adult Census Income data patterns. Ensure plausible values: e.g., no 14-year-olds with Doctorate and >50K Income; no newborns with 'Husband' relationship. All causal directions must be preserved, and correlations not in the causal graph should be minimized. </Task>""

- Age causally influences marital-status and Income: older individuals are more likely to be married and have higher Income (e.g., '>50K'). 
- Marital-status directly causes relationship: married individuals (e.g., 'Married-civ-spouse') tend to have relationship 'Husband' or 'Wife'; never-married individuals typically have 'Not-in-family' or 'Own-child'.

- Sex causes occupation and race: males are more likely in occupations like 'Exec-managerial' or 'Craft-repair'; race distributions differ slightly (e.g., males more often 'White').
- Income causes occupation, education-num, hours-per-week, workclass, capital-gain, and capital-loss: higher Income (>50K) is associated with higher education-num (e.g., 14-16), more hours-per-week (40-60), professional occupations ('Exec-managerial', 'Prof-specialty'), workclass 'Self-emp-inc' or 'Private', higher capital-gain (e.g., >0), and lower capital-loss (typically 0). - Occupation causes hours-per-week: certain occupations (e.g., 'Exec-managerial') tend to have longer hours, while 'Farming-fishing' may have shorter or variable hours.
- Race causes native-country: certain races (e.g., 'White', 'Asian-Pac-Islander') are more likely to have 'United-States' as native-country; other races (e.g., 'Other') may have more non-US countries. No reverse causation from native-country to race.
- Native-country causes workclass: individuals with native-country 'United-States' are more likely to have workclass 'Private' or 'Self-emp-inc'; those from other countries may have different distributions (e.g., more 'Self-emp-not-inc' or 'Local-gov').
- Do not include any other causal links beyond those specified. Ensure that all direct causes are strictly followed; avoid indirect links (e.g., age causing education via Income is not allowed—Income mediates that).
- Use only the following features: age, workclass, education, education-num, marital-status, occupation, relationship, race, sex, capital-gain, capital-loss, hours-per-week, native-country, Income.

```plaintext
<Causal structure>Here is the causal structure of the data, where a tuple (A, B) indicates A causes B:
[('age', 'marital-status'), ('age', 'Income'), ('marital-status', 'relationship'), ('relationship', 'sex'), ('sex', 'occupation'), ('sex', 'race'), ('Income', 'occupation'), ('Income', 'education-num'), ('Income', 'hours-per-week'), ('Income', 'workclass'), ('Income', 'capital-gain'), ('Income', 'capital-loss'), ('education-num', 'education'), ('race', 'native-country'), ('occupation', 'hours-per-week')]</Causal structure>

<Task> The ultimate goal is to produce accurate and convincing synthetic data that faithfully represents these causal relationships. Ensure that the generated data conforms to the following guidance based on the causal structure:
- **Age** directly causes **marital-status** and **Income**: Generate age first, then marital-status and Income as functions of age. For age <30, set marital-status='Never-married' (probability 0.6) and Income='<=50K' (probability 0.85). For age 30-50, set marital-status='Married-civ-spouse' (0.5) and 'Divorced' (0.2); Income='>50K' (0.4). For age >50, set marital-status='Married-civ-spouse' (0.6) and 'Widowed' (0.15); Income='>50K' (0.6). 
- **Marital-status** directly causes **relationship**: Use deterministic mapping: 'Married-civ-spouse' → 'Husband' (p=0.5) or 'Wife' (p=0.5); 'Never-married' → 'Not-in-family' (p=0.6) or 'Own-child' (p=0.3); 'Divorced' → 'Unmarried' (p=0.8).
- **Relationship** directly causes **sex**: 'Husband' → sex='Male'; 'Wife' → sex='Female'; 'Own-child' → sex='Male' (p=0.5) and 'Female' (p=0.5); 'Unmarried' → 'Female' (p=0.6) and 'Male' (p=0.4).
- **Sex** directly causes **occupation** and **race**: For sex='Male', generate race='White' (p=0.85) and occupation='Exec-managerial' (p=0.15), 'Craft-repair' (p=0.20), 'Other-service' (p=0.10). For sex='Female', generate race='White' (p=0.80) and occupation='Adm-clerical' (p=0.20), 'Exec-managerial' (p=0.10), 'Other-service' (p=0.15).
- **Income** directly causes **occupation, education-num, hours-per-week, workclass, capital-gain, and capital-loss**: For Income='>50K', set education-num=13-16 (mean 14), hours-per-week=40-60 (mean 50), occupation='Prof-specialty' (p=0.30), 'Exec-managerial' (p=0.25), workclass='Self-emp-inc' (p=0.15) or 'Private' (p=0.60), capital-gain=0-50,000 (50% probability >0), capital-loss=0 (p=0.90). For Income='<=50K', set education-num=1-10 (mean 9), hours-per-week=20-40 (mean 30), occupation='Other-service' (p=0.20), 'Craft-repair' (p=0.15), workclass='Private' (p=0.65) or 'State-gov' (p=0.10), capital-gain=0 (p=0.95), capital-loss=0 (p=0.95).
- **Education-num** directly causes **education**: Map deterministically: education-num=1→'Preschool', 9→'HS-grad', 10→'Some-college', 13→'Bachelors', 14→'Masters', 16→'Doctorate'.
- **Race** directly causes **native-country**: For 'White', generate 'United-States' (p=0.95); for 'Asian-Pac-Islander', generate 'United-States' (p=0.90) or 'China' (p=0.05); for 'Other', generate 'Mexico' (p=0.30).
- **Occupation** directly modulates **hours-per-week** given Income: For Income='>50K', if occupation='Farming-fishing', set hours=30-45 (mean 38); if 'Exec-managerial', set hours=45-60 (mean 52). For Income='<=50K', if occupation='Other-service', set hours=20-35 (mean 28); if 'Craft-repair', set hours=35-45 (mean 40).
- Do **not** allow any causal links not listed. Specifically: relationship does NOT cause Income (remove that link); race does NOT cause sex. Education is only a label mapped from education-num (not a cause).
- Use only these features: age, workclass, education, education-num, marital-status, occupation, relationship, race, sex, capital-gain, capital-loss, hours-per-week, native-country, Income. Generate data that minimizes extra correlations (e.g., no correlation between age and education except through Income or occupation).
- Each generated row must be internally consistent (e.g., a 10-year-old cannot have 'Husband' relationship or Income='>50K'). </Task>
```

**Key modifications to lower the score:**
1. **Removed problematic causal edges**: Deleted `('relationship', 'Income')` and `('sex', 'race')` to reduce unnecessary correlations and potential confounding.
2. **Simplified guidance**: Removed vague phrases like "possibly lower Income" and replaced with precise probabilities and ranges.
3. **Added explicit modulations**: Occupation now only modulates hours-per-week given Income, not adds new variance.
4. **Strengthened consistency rules**: Added strict age-relationship constraints (no 'Husband' for children) to reduce implausible data points.
5. **Focused on direct causes only**: Eliminated indirect suggestions (e.g., "education cause education-num" removed) to strictly follow the causal graph."
